ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY A1 AGRICULTURAL AND RURAL RESIDENTIAL TO MR MEDIUM-
DENSITY RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND LOCATED NORTHWEST OF THE 
INTERSECTION OF CHRISTENSEN ROAD AND PERSHING BOULEVARD.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on May 2, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as a portion of the east half 
of Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, more particularly described 
as follows:

of Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., of the 6th P.M., bears N35°18'49”E, a distance of 678.48 feet (said 
point of beginning, being the SE corner of Lot 11, Block 3, Saddle Ridge 16th Filing); thence

the east line of Section 25; thence along the east line of Section 25, N00°07'48”E, a distance of a

easterly line of Saddle Ridge 13th Filing for the following 19 courses; thence S00°07'26”W, a

feet; thence S39°48'12”E, a distance of 170.00 feet; thence N50°11'48”E, a distance of 140.00 feet;

of 90°00'00", having a radius of 20.00 feet, and whose long chord bears S44°51'38"E, a distance 
of 28.28 feet to a point; thence S89°51'38”E, a distance of 111.54 feet; thence 239.29 feet on a 
curve the right, through an angle of 31°53'04", having a radius of 430.00 feet, and whose long 
chord bears S73°55'06"E, a distance of 236.21 feet to a point; thence S57°58'35”E, a distance of

through an angle of 65°53'23", having a radius of 20.00 feet, and whose long chord bears 
N32°58'45"E, a distance of 21.75 feet to a point; thence N00°02'04”E, a distance of 21.52 feet; 
thence N68°52'12E, a distance of 64.34 feet; thence 39.83 feet along a curve to the left, through 
an angle of 114°06'37", having a radius of 20.00 feet, and whose long chord bears S57°01'15"E, a

Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00098-01-01: Saddle Ridge 18th Filing
Zoning Map Amendment
(assigning a City zone to annexed land)
REQUEST:

Agricultural and Rural Residential to City MR Medium-density Residential
LOCATION:
Northwest of the intersection of Christensen Road and Pershing Boulevard
APPLICANT:
WJE, LLC
AGENT:
John Sayers
OWNER:
WJE, LLC
ZONING:
County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential
FUTURE LAND USE:
Urban Residential
PURPOSE:

PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00098-01-01
May 2, 2022
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of the east half of Section 25, Township 14 North, Range 66 
West, of the 6th Principal Meridian to MR as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the 
project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County A-1 to City MR for 53.5 acres of 
land, concurrent with an annexation. The assignment of a City zone is necessary to establish the regulation of 
annexed property.  
 
The land is undeveloped. The impetus behind the annexation and zone change is to develop the property 
with a suburban, single-family residential development.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-22-00098-01-01
May 2, 2022
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is very close to the Urban Service Boundary. Infrastructure 
will need to be extended to service the property. 
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential. Surrounding areas to the 
east, west, and south are all designated Urban Residential. According to comprehensive plan, 
the optimal City zones 
include most of the 
residential zones, including MR. 
 
Areas designated Urban Residential are anticipated to develop with a broad range of housing 
types, and in newly developing areas of the community, some commercial uses are appropriate. 
Development is anticipated to be at an urban or suburban character with full access to urban 
services.   
 
The policies and future land use map of PlanCheyenne are supportive of this proposed zone 
assignment. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Surrounding property in the County is zoned various levels of agricultural. The land outside of 
City limits to the south and east are also separated from the subject property by major 
transportation corridors. The property to the north is occupied with a church. The adjacent 
properties within City limits are MR and P. The P zone is anticipated to develop with a school, 
and the MR zoned areas are anticipated in in a way similar to the proposed use of the subject 
property.  
 
This proposed zone change would allow this parcel to develop in a way similar to adjacent City-
jurisdiction property. The proposed zoning is also compatible with the future land use 
designations on nearby property. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property currently has a County zone, but in order to develop within the City, a City zone is 
required. The proposed MR zone would allow a variety of residential uses.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 

UDC-22-00098-01-01
May 2, 2022
The subject property is large enough that subdivision of the property, including public streets. 
The property will be required to provide connections to the existing street network and at least 
one stub out to the property to the north. A concurrent plat has been submitted with this zone 
change, demonstrating (in principle) the ability for this property to meet the standards of Article 
4 for the MR Medium-density Residential zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MR Medium-density Residential zone is intended to develop with a mix of dwelling types in 
a more suburban residential development pattern. The zone district is appropriate in areas 
designated Urban Residential by the future land use plan. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The proposed zone district is MR Medium-density residential, for a large acreage of land which 
is anticipated to be subdivided prior to construction. Should the specific subdivision proposed in 
concert with this zone change not be executed, the MR zone would allow a variety of residential 
uses and there is sufficient land to create other types of subdivision. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 

UDC-22-00098-01-01
May 2, 2022
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The MR zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County A-1 to City MR. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND LOCATED NORTH OF MISSILE DRIVE, EAST OF 
I-25.” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. 
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
That Capital Development, LLC, owners of: 
a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, 84 Lumber Company Site, Laramie County, 
Wyoming, more particularly described as follows: 
 
beginning at the Northwest corner of the Original City of Cheyenne, thence 
S26°18'30”E, along the West line of the Original City of Cheyenne, a distance 
of 569.48 feet to the North line of that parcel recorded In Book 2474, Page 
1144, thence S62°26'54”W, along said North Line, a distance of 117.76 feet to 
the West line of Lot 1; thence N23°40'06”W, along the West line, a distance 
of 372.32 to the Westerly corner of Lot 1; thence N0°22'38”E, a distance of 
223.97 feet to the point of beginning. Containing 1.16 acres more or less. 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; and 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Jeffrey B. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 5910; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned LI Light 
Industrial by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 9:47 pm, Apr 19, 2022

(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY
MAY 9, 2022
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00096: Annexation of 2600 Missile Drive (84 Lumber Company Site)
REQUEST:
An annexation of 1.2 acres of land
LOCATION:
North of Missile Drive east of I-25
APPLICANT:
Capital Development, LLC (Dan Montgomery)
AGENT:
Steil Surveying Services, LLC
Shane Hansen
OWNER:
Capital Development, LLC
PURPOSE:
To put the entire ownership parcel in one jurisdiction
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 1.2 acres of land to place the entire property under one jurisdiction. 
Currently, the property is divided between City and County jurisdiction. Despite this, the County Assessor 
presently shows both the City and County areas as one combined parcel. The land is currently developed in 
both jurisdictions with an industrial use. The user of the property desired to expand the business by placing a 
new building. A zone change is proposed concurrently with this annexation. Both applications are anticipated 
to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County LI Light Industrial to the City LI Light Industrial zone. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned LI Light Industrial in the County. The property is concurrently proposed 
to be annexed and rezoned to the City LI Light Industrial zone. The City LI zone is intended for 
environmentally controlled manufacturing or intensive employment uses that may have accessory 
commercial, service or office uses, but which cannot readily fit into other general business or 
mixed-use areas due to the nature or intensity of operations. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants. By 
being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and sanitation

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City sanitary sewer 
instead of having septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is largely surrounded by the City (the non-annexed area adjacent is 
mostly WYDOT right-of-way). The contiguity, the fact the ownership parcel is already partially in 
the City, and the proximity to surrounding urban-style development makes the parcel a natural 
geographic part of the City. The development of this property will benefit those who live within 
the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities already exist in the area. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 54% contiguous, along the eastern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
6)
The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on April 7, 2022. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for May 9, 2022. Twenty (20) business days prior to the 
announced public hearing is April 11, 2022. The earliest confirmation of delivery was dated April 
8, 2022. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage the maximization of existing infrastructure and the reduction of 
County pockets. This property is near existing urban infrastructure, and the annexation would effectively 
reduce a County pocket. 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with industrial uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access to 
urban services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
Location of Water and Sewer mains. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.

3. Applicant-submitted annexation map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY LI LIGHT INDUSTRIAL TO LI LIGHT INDUSTRIAL FOR LAND 
LOCATED NORTH OF MISSILE DRIVE, EAST OF I-25.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on May 2, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as a portion of Lot 1, Block 
1, 84 Lumber Company Site, Laramie County, Wyoming, more particularly described as follows:
beginning at the Northwest corner of the Original City of Cheyenne, thence 
S26°18'30”E, along the West line of the Original City of Cheyenne, a distance of 
569.48 feet to the North line of that parcel recorded In Book 2474, Page 1144, thence 
S62°26'54”W, along said North Line, a distance of 117.76 feet to the West line of Lot 
1; thence N23°40'06”W, along the West line, a distance of 372.32 to the Westerly 
corner of Lot 1; thence N0°22'38”E, a distance of 223.97 feet to the point of beginning. 
Containing 1.16 acres more or less.
is hereby changed to LI Light Industrial.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval, publication, and 
the recording of the annexation encompassing the area described in Section 4 with the County Clerk and 
Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of the Lot 1, Block 1, 84 Lumber Company Site to LI as shown 
in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County LI to City LI for 1.2 acres of land, 
concurrent with an annexation. The assignment of a City zone is necessary to establish the regulation of 
annexed property.  
 
The land is already developed with industrial use. The impetus behind the annexation and zone change is to 
jurisdictions).  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is very close to the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Policies in PlanCheyenne encourage industrial development near 
regional transportation routes and on the edges of the community (where they can be buffered 
from the residential and customer-based commercial uses). 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-22-00101
May 2, 2022
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Industrial. Surrounding areas are largely 
designated Industrial. According to comprehensive plan, the optimal City zones for the subject 
are LI and HI. 
 
Areas designated Industrial are anticipated to develop with employment uses which have 
potential negative impacts to surrounding properties. These areas should be located near major 
transportation routes, and should be buffered from residential areas. 
 
The policies and future land use map of PlanCheyenne are supportive of this proposed zone 
assignment. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
All surrounding property is zoned LI (either in the City or without). Both the City and County LI 
zones allow a mix of industrial, commercial, service, or office uses.  
 
This proposed zone change would allow this parcel to continue to match adjacent property. The 
proposed zoning is also compatible with the future land use designations on nearby property. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property currently has a County zone, but in order to develop within the City, a City zone is 
required. The proposed LI zone is analogous to the current County LI zone.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Block sizes are not applicable in areas designated industrial. Regional transportation routes 
severely limit the ability of this area to add to the street network. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The LI Light Industrial zone is intended to develop with limited manufacturing or intensive 
employment uses, located near major transportation corridors and buffered from residential 

UDC-22-00101
May 2, 2022
uses. It is applicable in areas designated Industrial or Mixed-use Employment Campus by the 
future land use map. 
 
The subject property is located near an interstate and a railroad and is more than 500 from the 
nearest residential zone (across a rail yard). The property is designated Industrial by the future 
land use map. 
 
Building design standards do not apply to the LI zone, and other design standards apply across 
zoning districts. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The reason for this rezoning is to assign a City zone in concert with an annexation. The 
annexation is proposed to put an entire facility into one jurisdiction. There is no substantive 
changes to the use of the property currently proposed. Should the property be redeveloped, 
there are a number of industrial and commercial uses which would be eligible to be established 
at this location. Having the property under one jurisdiction would provide regulatory clarity for 
future development. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 

UDC-22-00101
May 2, 2022
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The LI zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County LI to City LI.

1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d)

natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and

basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason,

its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.

the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.

the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.

severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared

remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 42.5 acres of land to develop the property with an entertainment 
use. Currently, the property is part of a much larger ownership parcel under County jurisdiction. The land is 
currently undeveloped. A zone change is proposed concurrently with this annexation. Both applications are 
anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County PUD (Swan Ranch) to City PUD (Swan Ranch  ORD 3989). 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned with an industrial-centered PUD zone in the County. The property is 
concurrently proposed to be annexed and rezoned to the City Swan Ranch PUD zone (ORD 3989). This 
PUD zone is intended to create an area for specialized commercial and industrial businesses in a rural 
setting where rail is the predominant reason businesses are locating in the area and where vehicular and 
pedestrian traffic will be highly impacted by train operations. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the west. Nearby properties are developed 
with industrial-park like development patterns. The contiguity and the proximity to surrounding 
urban-style development makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The 
development of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the 
social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities already exist in the area. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 25% contiguous, along the western edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on April 7, 2022. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for May 9, 2022. Twenty (20) business days prior to the 

UDC-22-00096
May 9, 2022
announced public hearing is April 11, 2022. The earliest confirmation of delivery was dated April 
8, 2022. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage responsible growth. As this annexation is on the edge of the 
community, any development action needs to take into careful consideration how to extend 
infrastructure; however, utilities and streets have largely already been built to practical extents.    
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with industrial uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access to 
urban services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
Water main extension 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Aerial, Current City Limits, and Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map

ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY

Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map

of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on May 2, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning

UDC-22-00117
May 2, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of the South Half of Section 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 
West of the 6th Principal Meridian to City Swan Ranch PUD (extending the regulating plan of Ordinance 
3989) as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for 
approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County PUD to City PUD (ORD 3989) for 
36.4 acres of land, concurrent with an annexation. The assignment of a City zone is necessary to establish the 
regulation of annexed property.  
 
The proposed City PUD zone is essentially a lightly modified version of the City HI Heavy Industrial zone. For 
the purposes of comparing the zoning, the analysis largely utilizes comparisons to a proposed zoning of HI. 
 
The land is currently vacant (although some limited agricultural operations  such as grazing  may be 
operating on the site). The impetus behind the annexation and zone change is an anticipated recreation use.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

Industrial 

UDC-22-00117
May 2, 2022
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is very close to the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Policies in PlanCheyenne encourage industrial development near 
regional transportation routes and on the edges of the community (where they can be buffered 
from the residential and customer-based commercial uses). 
 
The market opportunity associated with the rail line would encourage the development of more 
industrial uses in the of the Swan Ranch PUD. PlanCheyenne encourages the flexibility to be 
responsive to market demands. 
 
This property is part of a designated gateway to Cheyenne. Investment has been put into the 
Welcome Center and the I-25 and High Plains Road interchange to make a positive impact on 
those entering not only Cheyenne, but the State of Wyoming. Gateways are encouraged to 
provide an enhanced visual experience, including the use of landscaping. The proposed PUD 
reduces the amount of required landscaping; however, this is not necessarily a challenge with 
the proposed rezoning, as there would be potential to coordinate landscaping on this parcel to 
present attractive and quality landscaping closest to the interstate and interchange entrance 
into the facility. 
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Employment Campus. 
Surrounding areas are designated Industrial, Mixed-use Employment Campus, and Mixed-use 
Commercial. 
future land use designation MUE Mixed-use Employment. The proposed zoning is closer to HI 
zoning than Mixed-use Employment. Mixed-use Employment Campus areas are encouraged to 
develop with a primary focus on employment designed in a business campus setting. Parks, 
plazas, pedestrian amenities, and commercial uses are expected parts of development 
established under this designation. While the regional development pattern and proposed 
zoning appear antithetical to these objectives, the plan specifically uses Swan Ranch as an 
-Use Employment Campus (and the plan was adopted after the 
original Swan Ranch PUD was adopted). It is evident that the framers of PlanCheyenne 
considered the Swan Ranch Rail Park as an acceptable form of Employment Campus, and 
 
 
This property is also designated as a Community/Regional Activity Center on the Future Land 
Use Map. Activity Centers are intended for a mix of commercial and residential uses with access 
to regional transportation. Regional-serving retail is expected to be a major component of a 
Community/Regional Activity Center. The proposed zone would not allow the retail and 
residential components typically expected in activity centers. However, there is an allowance for 
varied activity center formats which respect the surrounding neighborhood; and there is 
potential for future development around the interchange to be more reflective of the kind of 
regional center anticipated by the future land use plan designation. 
 
The policies of PlanCheyenne are supportive of this proposed zone assignment. The future land 
use plan would encourage more commercial-style zoning at this location, but the current Swan 
Ranch area is a unique style of employment campus activity center clearly anticipated by the 
plan on the whole.  
 
The proposed zone change largely complies with review criterion 1. 
 

UDC-22-00117
May 2, 2022
2)
The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
All surrounding property within City are zoned with the Swan Ranch PUD proposed with this 
parcel. The subject property and all surrounding property outside of City limits west of I-25 are 
zoned with the County PUD. Both the City and County PUDs allow a variety of industrial uses, 
with some commercial, service, or office uses.  
 
This proposed zone change would allow this parcel to match adjacent property. The proposed 
zoning is also compatible with the future land use designations on property west of I-25. I-25 
and the railroad act as a buffer between the development on this property and any proposed 
developments on the east side of I-25. Currently, all property east of I-25 is zoned for 
agricultural use and is largely undeveloped. 
 
To the south, there is the WYDOT welcome center. This property is adjacent to I-25, a railroad 
track, and an area already zoned with an industrial PUD. This zoning would not have an undue 
influence on this use. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property currently has a County zone, but in order to develop within the City, a City zone is 
required. The proposed PUD zone is analogous to the current County PUD zone.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Block sizes are not applicable in areas designated mixed-use employment or industrial. Access 
may be limited based on street classification and proximity to the interstate, but specific access 
would be reviewed with plat(s) or site plan(s). Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the 
area. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
This PUD is intended to develop with a more rural commercial and industrial development 
pattern. The area is anticipated to be heavily impacted by rail operations. Anticipated uses are 
those which are of a very high intensity which should be isolated and buffered from the 
community. 
 

UDC-22-00117
May 2, 2022
Within the PUD, design standards do not apply to industrial uses (PUD Section C); however, non-
industrial uses would be expected to meet the standards of UDC 6.7 (per UDC 6.7.2.d). 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed in concert with this rezoning is a recreation facility. Should 
the proposed development not be executed after approval, there are also a number of industrial 
and commercial uses which would be eligible to be established at this location. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The PUD zone is 
appropriate based on the comprehensive plan, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  

UDC-22-00117
May 2, 2022
2.
Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County PUD to City PUD. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. City Swan Ranch PUD Regulating Plan (from ORD 3989) 
2. Current Zoning Map 
3. Future Land Use Map 
4. Master Application 
5. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS 
FOR 
LAND 
LOCATED 
NORTHEAST 
OF 
THE 
INTERSECTION OF DELL RANGE BOULEVARD AND RIDGE ROAD.” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, 
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
Section 2. That on May 2, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zone change. 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as: Lot 1, Dell Range Marketplace, 3rd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming, is hereby changed to CB Community Business. 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
MAY 2, 2022 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-22-00121:  3410 Dell Range Zone Change  
 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 1.16 Acres of land from MUB Mixed-
use Business to CB Community Business. 
LOCATION: 
Northeast corner of Dell Range Blvd and Ridge Road. 
AGENT: 
OWNER: 
Don Miller 
John Preiss, McDonald’s 
ZONING: 
MUB Mixed-use Business  
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Mixed-use Commercial 
PURPOSE: 
Rezoning to CB to allow a pole sign and to match existing development 
standards. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II - Development  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-22-00121 
 
 
May 2, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Dell Range Marketplace, 3rd Filing to CB Community Business, 
noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
• 
Harmony Valley PUD 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB Community 
Business. The land is currently developed as a McDonalds. The purpose of the proposed zone change is to 
match the current development pattern and allow a pole sign to be reconstructed.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. The Mixed‐Use 
Commercial category is intended to promote a range of land uses, with primarily retail, office, 
light industrial, and livework designed as an “activity center”. Parks, plazas and/or open space 
should also be part of the core of Mixed‐Use Commercial areas. 
 
The existing development is a restaurant which would fall into the commercial portion of the 
designation. The proposed CB zone better facilitates the type of development on this lot.   
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Mixed-use Commercial  
MUB (Mixed-use Business) Restaurant  
North 
Mixed-use Commercial 
MUB   
Commercial  
South 
Community Business 
CB (Community Business) 
Automobile Service 
Station 
East 
Mixed-use Commercial 
MUB 
Service Business  
West 
Mixed-use Residential  
CB 
  Restaurant  

UDC-22-00121 
 
 
May 2, 2022 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
There is CB zoning to the South and to the West of this property. The surrounding area consists 
of a mix of different commercial and service uses. The CB zone would be compatible with the 
nearby properties as it is a commercial zone with slightly different standards than the MUB 
zone.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The existing development matches the standards of CB zone better than the MUB zone. The 
MUB zone would have required the building to be set closer to the streets, whereas the CB zone 
pushes buildings further from the street. The CB zone is also meant for more vehicle-oriented 
areas which would be the case for Dell Range Blvd.   
   
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
This is an existing development which met the requirements of Article 4 when constructed 
under the MUB zone and would have to meet the same requirements with the proposed CB 
zone. Should the property ever redevelop under the CB zone, it would be able to meet the 
standards of Article 4.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas. The existing development is a 
restaurant which meets the intent of the CB zone. 
 
The CB District is applicable in areas where moderate- or large-scale business uses are 
appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional 
Activity Centers, or Community Business areas of the Comprehensive Plan. 
 

UDC-22-00121 
 
 
May 2, 2022 
The existing development better meets the design objectives of the CB zone than the current 
MUB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The existing building meets the setback requirements of the CB zone, and should any future 
development occupy the site, they will also be able to meet the setbacks. By zoning the property 
CB the property will be more conforming with the UDC.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major concerns were raised by other revieing bodies. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The proposed 
CB zone brings the existing property closer to compliance with the Unified Development Code.  
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00121 
 
 
May 2, 2022 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. (Current) Future Land Use Map 
3. Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM CB 
COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO MR MEDIUM-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL FOR 
LAND LOCATED SOUTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF OMAHA ROAD 
AND PINE DRIVE.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on May 2, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lot 1, Block 1, Potter 
Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to MR Medium-density Residential.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00131: 3173 Omaha Zoning Map Amendment 
(zone change)
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 0.34 acres of land from CB 
Community Business to MR Medium-density Residential
LOCATION:
Southeast of the intersection of Omaha Road and Pine Drive
APPLICANT:
Meadowbrook Baptist Church
AGENT:
Scott Catellier
OWNER:
Meadowbrook Baptist Church
ZONING:
CB Community Business
FUTURE LAND USE:
Urban Residential
PURPOSE:
To allow the property to be used as a residence
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00131
May 2, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot1, Block1, Potter Addition to MR Medium-density Residential as 
shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business to MR Medium-
density Residential for 0.34 acres of land. The land is currently occupied by a structure (formerly used for 
business purpose). The property is anticipated to be reused for residential purposes.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development (although it is not the 
intent of the plan to change existing, stable neighborhoods).   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential. Areas to the west, east, 
and south are also designated Urban Residential. 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Urban Residential   
CB Community Business 
Existing structure

Urban Residential, 
Community Business

Residential, LR Low-density 
Residential

Urban Residential

Assembly

Urban Residential    
MR 
Single-family residential

Urban Residential

Single-family residential 

UDC-22-00131
May 2, 2022
According to comprehensive plan the optimal City zone for the
future land 
use designation is any one of the more urban residential zone districts, including the MR zone. 
Urban residential areas are encouraged to develop with a variety of residential uses, and the 
zoning of this parcel would allow residential use. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, MR zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Surrounding property to the east and south are currently zoned with the MR zone designation. 
Property to the west (across Omaha) is zoned LR. This proposed zone change would allow this 
parcel to match adjacent property. These surrounding parcels are also designated urban 
residential by the future land use map in PlanCheyenne, anticipating the continuation of 
residential uses.  
 
The property to the north is diagonally off of the site and zoned CB and is developed for 
commercial use. The proposed MR zone would not present an undue influence on this property 
(especially as there are other properties closer to this CB parcel zoned for residential use. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is currently zoned CB, which requires a landscape buffer to adjacent residential 
zones. Were this property to redevelop in the CB zone, a significant portion of the site would be 
constrained by the required buffers. The CB zone is intended to develop with largely commercial 
uses, although limited multi-family residential is also possible. The MR zone mostly allows 
residential uses  though some small-scale, low-intensity non-residential uses are also 
applicable. Due to location, it appears probable the property is more suited to residential 
development.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The zone change is occurring in a pre-existing MR-zoned neighborhood. The block is much too 
large to meet the standards for residential development, but there are no real options to create 
meaningful street network connections. No additions to the street network are proposed.  
 
Omaha is a major road and it appears there may be a curb cut directly to the property. This may 
need to be reviewed, as it appears there is potential to access the parcel from the neighboring 
property (and both parcels are currently under the same ownership).  

UDC-22-00131
May 2, 2022
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the area. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MR zone is intended for a mix of residential uses in a more urban environment. The area 
around the parcel to the south and west is already zoned MR and is largely compatible with the 
intent and applicability statements of the MR zone. MR zoning is anticipated to meet the design 
standards of Section 6.6 of the UDC. Any redevelopment of this property should be able to meet 
these standards. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed in concert with this rezoning is the repurposing of the 
existing structure for a single-family residence. Should the proposed development not be 
executed after approval, there are also a number of residential uses which would be eligible to 
be established at this location. Also, the MR zone would still allow some limited commercial 
uses (such as a small law office). 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 

UDC-22-00131
May 2, 2022
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the MR 
zone at this location. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The MR zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated.

Governing Body approve this application by a unanimous vote.

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED:
“AN ORDINANCE VACATING A PORTION OF THE 
GOODNIGHT TRAIL ROUNDABOUT, SWEETGRASS, 1ST
FILING, CITY OF CHEYENNE, LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING (LOCATED ALONG GOODNIGHT TRAIL, 
BETWEEN COLLEGE DRIVE AND MURRAY ROAD)”.
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
Section 1.
That the City Right-of-Way (ROW) property described as a 4,784 square foot 
portion of the east leg of the Goodnight Trail Roundabout, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing, City of Cheyenne,
Laramie County, Wyoming, is hereby vacated as shown on the attached Vacation Map.
Section 2.
The vacation of the 4,784-square feet of undeveloped ROW is in the best interest 
of the City of Cheyenne and its residents.
Section 3.
That said vacated right-of-way shall revert to the adjacent property owners pursuant 
to Wyoming State Statutes.
Section 4.
The said vacated land shall have no easements, reservations or restrictions of record 
unless shown on the attached Vacation Map.
Section 5.
The City Clerk is directed to record this ordinance, together with the Vacation Map, 
with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.
Section 6.
That the City Engineer’s office is directed to change the Official City Map to reflect 
this vacation and the Official Zoning Map designating the vacation request for the properties as indicated 
as zone Sweetgrass PUD (Ordinance 4286 and subsequent amendments).
Section 7.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, 
paragraphs and sections.
Section 8.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00124: Goodnight Trail roundabout Vacation
REQUEST:
To vacate the east leg of the Goodnight Trail roundabout right-of-way 
originally dedicated with Sweetgrass, 1st Filing and lying adjacent to Lot 1, 
Block 1, Sweetgrass 4th Filing between College Drive and Murray Road
LOCATION:
East of Goodnight Trail, between College Drive and Murray Road
APPLICANT:
Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC
AGENT:
Palma Land Planning
PURPOSE:
To give more control over the entry to the commercial development 
benefitting therefrom 
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd Planner II - Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00124
May 2, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the vacation of the east 47.84 feet (4,784 square feet) of the Goodnight Trail 
roundabout, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.1.4 Street Vacation 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The Goodnight Trail and Sweetgrass Drive roundabouts serve as the entry points for a commercial space 
adjacent to College Drive within the Sweetgrass PUD. The owner of the commercial land has indicated the 
development would be better served by including more of the legs of the roundabout within the common 
parking and open space parcel. The vacation would allow the owners to have more control over the access 
point and 
 adversely affect the ability to 
access the commercial parcel.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Street Vacation, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.4.d.  A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The vacation meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4.  (UDC 
2.1.4.d.1) 
 
Article 4 of the Unified Development Code is generally silent on right-of-way vacations of roads 
outside of downtown. 
and lots are served by necessary infrastructure services, including utilities, transportation, storm 
drainage, public safety, and community facilities, but recognize that necessary service levels 
The right-of-way for 
vacation does not serve as a connection, and utilities utilizing this right-of-way will have 
easements provided.      
Future Land Use Designation 
Zoning 
Land Use 
Area proposed 
for vacation 
Mixed-use Commercial 
Sweetgrass PUD 
Roundabout entry  
North 
Mixed-use Commercial 
Sweetgrass PUD 
Parking and open space
South
